का०मा० 82 5.-- चूंकि केन्द्र सरकार का विचार है कि यह लोकहित की 
वृष्टि से भावश्यक एवं समीचीन होगा कि उन निर्धारितियों के नाम 
और अन्य विवरण प्रकाशित कर दिए जाएं, जिनका निर्धारण 
वित्तीय वर्ष 1979- 80 के दौरान न० 10 लाख से अधिक शुद्ध धन के 
लिए धनकर अधिनियम, 1957 ( 1957 का 27 ) के अधीन किया गया 
है, अत: धनकर अधिनियम 1957 ( 1957 का 27 ) की धारा 420 
बारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों और इस संबंध में इसे समर्थ बनाने वाली अन्य 
सभी शक्तियों के प्रयोग में एसद्वारा उपरोक्त निर्धारितियों के नाम मौर अन्य 
विवरण नीचे दिए गए अनुसार प्रकाशित किए जाते हैं । 

( 1 ) हैसियत के लिए व्यक्ति, हि० अ० प० व्यक्तियों का संगम 
( 2 ) निर्धारण वर्ष के लिए 
( 3) विवरणित शुर धन के लिए 
( 4 ) निर्धारित शुख धन के लिए 
( 5 ) देयकर के लिए 

( 6 ) अदा किए गए कर के लिए

21 ) की धारा 11 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग 
करते हुप, केन्द्रीय सरकार, एवद्वारा श्री ललित कुमार गौड़ को अलवर 
भरतपुर प्राचलिक ग्रामीण बैंक , भरतपुर का अध्यक्ष नियुक्त करती है तथा 
28- 2-1981 से प्रारम्भ होकर 27- 2- 1984 को समाप्त होने वाली अवधि

इस सूचना के प्रति पाप का उत्तर के आप बचाव के लिए अपेक्षित दस्ता 
वेजी साक्ष्य के साथ अधोहस्ताक्षरी को निर्धारित अवधि के भीतर पहुंच 
जाना चाहिए और ऐसा न करने की स्थिति में यह समझ लिया जाएगा 
कि पाप को इस संबंध में कुछ नहीं कहना है और इस मामले में गुण 
दोष के प्राधार पर एक तरफा निर्णय ले लिया जाएगा । यदि प्राप 
चाहते हैं कि पाप स्वयं पा कर मामले के बारे में बताएं तो व्यक्तिगत 
मुनवाई के लिए भी आपको अवसर प्रवान किया जाएगा । यदि आप इस अवसर 
का लाभ उठाना चाहते हो तो आप को चाहिए कि आप अपने प्रबंधफ / 
भागीदार/ स्वामी को व्यक्तिगत सुनवाई के लिए इस सूचना में संकेतित 
स्थान और पते पर प्रधोहस्ताक्षरी को मिलने के लिए बुधवार 11 फरवरी 
1981 सायं 3 . 30 बजे भेज दें । यदि आप इस प्रयोजन के लिए उक्त 
मारीग्ध और समय पर उपस्थित होने में असमर्थ होते हैं तो माप को 
पागामी सूचना दिए बिना ही रिकार्ड में उपलब्ध साक्ष्य के आधार पर 
मामले पर निर्णय ले लिया जाएगा । 
___ यदि आपके पास कोई उत्तर हो तो उसके साथ आपको उपर्युपत्त मूल 
लाइसेंस के अनुलिपि प्रितियों के साथ भेजने भी आवश्यक हैं । यदि प्राप 
ने इसका अनुपालन न किया तो पायात तथा निर्यात नियंत्रण अधिनियम , 
1947 के अधीन और इसके अधीन जारी किए गए आदेशों के अन्तर्गत 
उचित दण्ठनीय कार्रवाई की जाएगी ।

कि लोकहित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में जी० जी० एम० घोषित किया है । 
सोभासण II से सी० टी० एफ० सोभासण तफ पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के ___ अर्शत कि उक्त भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के नीचे 
लिये पाल्पलाइन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस पायोम डारा मिछाई आमी चाहिए । पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिए आक्षेप सक्षम अधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 

और यतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन गैस मायोग, निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड , पडोदरा - 9 
के लिये एतत्पाबद्ध अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार अर्जित को इस अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 
करमा मावश्यक है ।

का०आ० 83 3:--- यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है 
कि लोकहित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में जी जी० एम . 
सोभामण II मे मी०टी०एफ० सोभामण तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन 
के निये पाणलाइन तेम तथा प्राकृतिक गैम प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी 


वरासे कि उक्त भूमि में हितमन कोई व्यमित, उम भूमि के नीचे 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी , मेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
मैम प्रायोग, निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड, वडोदरा- 9 को 
म अधिसूचना की तारीख में 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 


चाहिए । 


और, यतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि प्रेमी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिये एतद्गायन अनुसूर्च, में वर्णिन भूमि म उपयोग का अधिकार अर्जित 
करना प्रावश्यक है । 


और ऐसा प्राक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टम: यह भी फमन 
करेगा कि क्या वह यह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई पनिगम हो या 
किमी विधि बसायी को मार्फत । 


अनः प्रम पेट्रोलियम और निज पाइपलाइन ( ममि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का भजन ) अधिनियम , 1 16:: ( 19 , 2 का 50) की धाग

7 . According to Mr. P . Das Gupta from the time of 
previous owner of the colliery , namely , Equitable Coal 
Company Ltd and for a long period the underground 
loaders were entitled to get 50 P per tub as difficulty 
allowance for working in deep water is a condition of 
service and that the said workmen had been receiving the 
said allowance all along till 1 -6 - 76 when the management 
of the colliery refused to make payment of that allowanco 
to the underground loaders. A long list of workman has 
boen given describing thera as underground loaders who 
according to the Union are cntitled to the difficulty allow 
ancc as claimed . On tho other hand Mr. Choudbury s 
contention is that according to the evidenco no difficulty 
allowance was ever puid ay alleged cjther before the 
imionalisation of the mines or thereafter. According to 
the amicable agreement hetween the workmen and the 
management of the Colliciy it was agreed that the under 
urolind loaders working in the Main Dip will do extra 
work for dressing and lifting out cval after digging and 
shovelling and if that extra work is done by any under -, 
ground loader he would be getting 50 P per tub as allow 
ance for such extra work and this allowance is called Main 
Dip allowanco shortly stated MD allowance. It was set 
tled that the underground loaders would be entitled to get 
this allowance for extra work to be done from 8th June, 
1974 . Mr. Choudhury has further argued that according 
to the evidence from July 1976 the workmer coased to do 
extra jon of lifting coal after digging and shovelling and 
they did only the job of dressing the cool. For this work 
of dressing . (loldg less extra work , they were paid at the 
rate of 34 P per tub and this extra allowance is being paid 
since then . The 4117 and substance of Mr. Choudhury s 
submission is that by the evidence adduced by the Colliery . 
both oral and documentary , the story of difficulty allowance 
set up by the workmen has been disproved and as such the 
workmen can get no relief here .

to the written statement of the Union . 


3 . To be brief, the case ol the Union is that the under 
ground loaders of the Colliery used to get 50 paise as the 
difficulty allowanco for vorking in water for a long time, 
even prior to nationalisation of conl mincs in 1973. The 
said allowance was one of the conditions of service . The 
management of the Collicry all on a sudden storped pay . 
ment of this difficult water allowance with effect from 
1 - 7 - 1976 without complying with tho provisions of Section 
9A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and without any 
reason . Tho Union ruised objection and demanded COD 
tinuance of the payment of the said allowance but the 
management refused to pay hood to the demand. In the 
circumstances the dispute was taken to the Assistant Labour 
Commissioner (Central ) and the matter has ultimately come 
to this Tribunal for decision . According to the Union 
there has been a violation of Section 9A of the lacustril 
Disputes Act by the Colliery for alteratitud of the condi 
tions of service by stopping the payment of difficulty 
allowance and their priyer is that the difficulty lowance 
should be paid from 1 - 7 -1976 .

cient. The underground loaders at the Main Dir agreed 
to perform the job of dressing as well as that of lifting 
out coal aftor digging and shovelling from th June, 1974 
which would be an extra work and for which the said 
workmen would be paid an allowance of 50 p per tub 
and the said allowance was called Main Dip (MD ) allow 
ance. From July 1976 the underground louders at the 
Main Dip of the Collicry ccased to do trocytra job of 
lifting out coal but continued to perform the job of 
dressing the qual only . If these circunstances they were 
being paid an allowance at the rate of 34 p per tub for 
not doing the job of lifting, diggiog or shovelling but for 
doing the work of dressing only . Those working in other 
galleries of the Colliery and noi doing the work of dressers 
were not paid any MD allowance . No ondes - ground loncler 
has a right to the allowance for extra work clone at the 
Tate of 50 P per tub from 1st June. 1976 when they ccascd 
to do the liſting out coal ulter digging and shovelling. The 
Union s case of plyment of difficulty allowance either 
prior or subsequent to nationalisation of coa ) nincs has 
been totally denied either as condition of service or

condition of service of the violation of Section 9A of 
the Industrial Disputes Act has also been denice

coal by digging and shovelling . The evidence further shows 
that subsequently as the loaders refused to lift coal by digging 
and shovelling, the Colliery started giving allowance at the 
rate of 34 P per tub only for drossing and this payment of 
dressing allowance at the rate of 34 P has been admitted 
by the witnesses on the side of the Union . Moreover, those 
witnesses have stated that they claimed difficulty allowance 
which is quite distinct from this allowance at the rate of 
34 P for dressing. The evidence is quite clear and it has 
been proved quite convincingly that at no point of time was 
there any allowance paid to the underground loaders towards 
difficulty allowance or otherwise for working in water. This 
payment was never made either during the time of erstwhile 
management or after nationalisation of the mine . Tho claim 
of the workman is frivolous and without any bagja at all. 
The case of the Colliery iş supported by reliable evidence 
both oral and documentary whereas the evidence on the 
side of the Union is thoroughly unacceptable. The case of 
the Union has been disproved and I find the claim for diffi 
culty allowance at the rate of 50 paise per tub is untepablo 
and unjustified . The workmen are not entitled to any relief 
whatsoever .

tion 7A and clauso ( d ) of sub -section ( 1 ) of section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby constitutes an Industrial Tribunal of 
which Shri T . Sudersanam Danial shall be the Presiding Officer, 
with headquarters at Madras and refers the said dispute for 
adjudication to the said Tribunal.

का०मा० 847. -- केन्द्रीय सरकार की राय है कि इससे उपायड मनसूची 
में विनिर्दिष्ट विषय के बारे में यूनियन बैंक प्राफ इंडिया , एरनाकुलम 
के प्रबन्ध -मंउल से सम्बद्ध एक प्रौद्योगिक विवाद नियोजकों और उनके 
फर्मकारों के बीच विद्यमान है । 

मौर केन्द्रीय सरकार उक्त विवाद को न्यायनिर्णयन के लिये निर्देशित 
करना यांछनीय समझती है । 

प्रतः, केन्द्रीय सरकार, प्रौद्योगिक विवाय अधिनियम , 1947 ( 1947 
का 14 ) की धारा 7-क और धारा 10 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के खंड ( प ) 
द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए , एक प्रौद्योगिक प्रधिकरण 
गठित करती है जिसके पीठासीन अधिकारी श्री टी० सुन्दरनम् डेनियल 
होंगे जिनका मुख्यालय मद्रास में होगा और उक्त विवाद को उक्त प्रौद्योगिक 
अधिकरण को न्यायनिर्णयन के लिये निर्देशित करती है । 

अनुसूची 
" क्या यूनियन बैंक माफ इरिया के भूतपूर्व चपरासी श्री सी० श्रीकुमारन 
नायर , को विकास कार्यालय , त्रिवेंद्रम से पदच्युत करने की कार्रवाई 
न्यायोचित है ? यवि नहीं , तो संबंधित फर्मकार किस अनुतोष का 
हकवार है ? "

AWARD 
By Order No. L - 20012 / 87 / 78 -DIII( A ) dated the Sth De 
cember , 1976 the Central Government being of opinion that 
an industrial dispute existed betwecn the cmployers in rela 
tion to the management of M / s. Bharat Coking Coal Limited , 
Arca No. V , Post Office Sijua , District Dhanbad and their 
workmen in respect of the matters specified in the schedule 
attached to the order , referred the same for adjudication to 
this Tribunal, The schedule to the order which is in two parts 
reads thus : 


2 . Wer notice to the parties they have filed their respective 
written statements and rejoinders , la course of hearing no oral 
evidence has been adduced by the management which has 
only filed four documents on which it relics. These documents 
have been marked as Exts. M - 1 , M - 2 , M - 2 / 1 and M - 3 on 
admission , On behalf of thc union the concerned workman has 
only been cxaminel. The union also has rclied upon aş many 
as seven documents marked as Ext, W - 1 , W - 2 , W - 3 , W -4 , 
W -4 / 1 , W - 5 nd W -6 on admission . The concerned workman 
examined as WW - 1 says that he started working in Loyabad 
Colliery since 1971 as an Attendance Clerk , From Loyabad 
Colliery hc was transferred to Sub - Area in 1973 us a Sales 
Assistant. Both as an Attendance Clerk and as a Sales Assis 
tant he was getting the pay of a clerk in Gr. Il. At the time 
of his transfer to Sub - Area office thc Sub - Area was under 
Area No . li. Thereafter Arca No . II was changed to Area 
No. V . liom Loyabad Sub - Area No, II he was transferred to 
Area No . V as Sales Assistant. From Arc No . V he was 
transforred to Munidih Project in December , 1976 . The work 
man asserts that after his transfer to Muinidih the post which 
he was holding in the Area Office was filled up by a person 
transferred from Coal Board . The transferee was in Gr. I. 

The reference in the present case consists of two parts . 
The llist part relates to a demand of the union on behalf 
of the concerned workman that the workman should have 
been given Gr. II with effect from August, 1973 . The sccond 
pirt relating to the demand of the union on behalf of the 
concerned workman is that the workman should have been 
considercal for promotion to Gr, I by D . P . C . which considered 
candidatca for promotion to that grade in May, 1977. The ad 
inilted position is that the concerned workman was confirmed 
in Gride II on 6th December , 1974 . On the date when the 
concerned workman joined for the first time in Loyabad Col 
liery in 1971 ih : colliery was under private management. The 
siid colliery was taken over on 17th October, 1971 . The 
Form B Register maintained by the private owners shows 
that the concerned workman was appointed with effcct from 
1st October, 1971 in a clerical post in Gr. III. This position 
also is not disputed . The workman however asserts in his 
evidence which is also not disputed that since the date of 
take over i.e . 171h October, 1971 he was working as an 
Attendance Clerk which belongs to Gr. II, According to 
workinan while he was working as attendance clerk in Gr. II 
he was transferred to Sub -Area Office as a Sales Asstt. which 
was also a clerical post in Gr. II , This position is also not 
challenged by the management, According to the workman 
from Sub - Are Office he was transferred to Arca Office at 
Sijya ay Sales Assistance which was also a clerical post in 
Gr. 11. This position is also udmitted by parties. Thereafter 
as per the evidence of the workman ho was transferred to 
Munidih Project linder Office Order Ext. W - 1 and he was 
releived from Area Ofllcc on 6th December , 1976 as per 
Office Order Ext. W - 2 . On his transfer to Munidih Project 
he also held a clerical post Gr. II. Thus it is scen that although 
thc Form B Register shows that the concerned workman was 
appointed on 1st Oct. 1971 as clerk in Grade Yll from 17th 
Oct, 1971 right Lipto his transfer to Munidih Project he has 
been holding the post of a clerk in GI, IT, The management 
Jose not dispute that for the entire period the workman work 
cd as a clerk in Gr. II, he was being paid the differences bet 
ween the pay and allowances of a Gr, IJ Clerk and those of 
a clerk in Gr. IIJ . In other words inspite of the fact that 
Form B Register shows that the concerned workman was 
anpointeil on 1st Oct. 1971 as a clerk in Gr. III he has been 
all along posted in a clerical post belonging to Gr, II and 
drawing the ray and allowances of a clerical post in Gr. II. 
In other words from 17th Oct. 1971 unto 6th Dec , 1976 when 
The concerned workman was released from Area Office to 
join his post in Munidih Project he has been working in a 
clerical post in Gr. II, This period comes to more than 5 


(CTT II 


** * 3 ( 1 ) ] 


Arta Ft 719a : 14 , 1981/ AFTTT 23 , 1902 


891 


years. It cannot be said that for this long 5 years the concern 
ed workrnan was mercly officiating or deputising for another 
personi holding a clerical post in Gr. II. Thę inacvitable con 
clusion therсtoro is since the date of iako over till the con 
cerned workman was transferred to Muniuih Project and even 
after the tranşter to Munidih Project the management has 
allowed the concerned workman to work in the post of a 
clerk in Gr. 11 and bas paid him the salary and allowances of 
a clerical post in Gr. 11, True a letter of confirmation has 
been issued to the concerned workman on 6th Dec, 1974 by 
the management confirmating him in a clerical post in Gr. II. 
There is absolutely no basis for this order . Nothing has been 
shown to indicate that a vacancy Occurred in the cadre of 
clerical post u Gr. Il on 6th Dec . 1974 as a rcsult of which 
the con erned workman was confirmed . No evidence has been 
led by the management to show the strength of clerical posts 
in Gr. II and the vacancies occuring in the cadre of clerical 
post in Gr, II from time to time. No reason has been assign 
ed by the management as to under whut circumstances tho 
concerned workman was confirmed only on 6th Dec . 1974 
and could not be confirmed carlier. The claim of the con 
cerned workman however is that he should have been con 
firmed in August 1973 and not on 6th December , 1974 as 
has been done by the management. The reason for this dc 
mand is that in August 1973 thc Loyabad Sub - Area Office 
was formed and the concerned workman was transferred from 
that office to the colliery , As has been indicated above even 
before the transfer to Sub - Area Office the concerned workman 
was holding the post of an Attendance Clerk belonging to 
Or. II , In that view thc workman could have claimed con 
firmation with effect from 17th October , 1971. But in view 
of the fact that the claim for confirmation is only from 
August, 1973 , therc is no reason why he should be denied 
confirmation at least from August 1973, in the absence of 
anything showa as indicated above that there was no perma 
nent post in the cadre of clerical Gr. II available in August, 
1973 , I, therefore , hold that the concerned workman is en 
titled to confirmation as clerk Gr. II with effect from August, 
1973 and the claim to this effect is justified . 


Coming to the other part of the demand mentioned in the 
schedule attached to the reference the case of tho union is 
that after the formation of the Arca Office the management 
adopted a policy for promotion of clerks from one grade to 
the next higher grade in the Arca . Office . The policy adopted 
by the management is Ext. M - 1 dated 18th May , 1977 . Para 1 
of Ext. M - 1 reads thus : 
" There should not be any upgradation within 4 years 

of the last upgradation / promotion / categorisation , 
This period of 4 years could be relaxed by six 
inonths , the maximum , in case of very good perform 
ance of employee , as per report of the departmental 
hcad ." 


the union which contains the minutes of a meeting between 
the management and the union sponsoring the cause of the 
concerned workman , It is not disputed that in thc meeting the 
claim of the concerned workman which is the subject matter 
of the piesent reference was discussed. Para 1 ot Ext. W - 3 
shows that it was agreed between thc parties present in the 
meoting that the case of the conccrocd workman would be 
considered for promotion by the D . P . C . in Area No . V on 
the basis as if the concerned workmun was not transferred . 
It was further agreed that if the concerned workman would 
be l ouod fit after consideration by the D . P . C . for promotion , 
he would be promoted . The genuineness of Ext. W - 3 is not 
disputed by the management. It is however argued on behalf 
of the management that after the agreement as per Ext. W - 3 
the case of the concerncd workman was considered and as 
it was found that he came under the exception as provided in 
pulu 1 of Ext , M - 1 thc D . P . C . did not consider the case of 
the concerned workman . Mr, T . P . Choudhury learned coun : 
set for the management invites my attention to the words 
" up- gradation / promotion / categorisation appearing in Ext. M - 1 
atid argues that under the expression provided in para 1 of 
EXI, IV - 1 if the post held by a workman has been up -graded 
wiunin 4 years or the workman has been promoted to a post 
within 4 years or the post held by the workman has been 
categoriscd as belonging to a particulur grade within 4 years 
iho U . P . C . would not consider such cases for promotion . This 
content.on of Mr. T . P . Choudhury is without any force . 
The post in which the concerned workman was holding right 
from 17th October , 1971 upto the date when the disputo 
arose was a clerical post in Grade II. So it could not bo said 
that the post which the concerncd workman wag holding was 
up - graded within 4 years from the date when the D . P . C . 
considered the cases of promotion . It is also not the case of 
any of the parties that the concerned workman was promoted 
to a clerical post in Gr. II within 4 years from the date when 
the D . P . C . sat to consider caşcs of promotion . It is also no 
body s case that the post which the concerned workman 
was holding from 17th October, 1971 was categorised as 
clerical post Gr. II within 4 years when the D . P . C mct. 
My conclusion , therefore, is that the case of the concerned 
workman could not be cxcluded from consideration for pro 
inotion because under para 1 of Ext. M - 1 his caso was to 
be excluded . I have already hold that there is no justification 
for management why it confirmed the concerned workman in 
clerical post Gr. II on 6th December, 1974 and not before 
that. At the cost of repetition I may say here that had tho 
management produced records to show the cadre strengths of 
cicrical posts in different grades in Area Office No . V and 
would have shown that the concerned workman could not 
have been confirmed beforo 6th December , 1974 as there 
was no vacancy then only I would have accepted the case of 
the management. That apart para 1 of Ext. M - 1 does not 
show that before a clerk in any particular grade is considered 
for promotion to the next grade ho must have been confirmed 
in the lower grade . Therefore according to me the date of 
confirmation is immaterial since as a matter of fact tho 
concerned workman was holding the post of a clerk in Gr. II 
for more than 5 years by the time D .P . C . met. Without any 
thing being shown it cannot be said that the past which the 
concerned workman was holding was in temporary or offi 
ciating capacity only . It is the normal rule in Government 
Offices that when a Governinent employee officiates in a post 
and is confirmed in the said post later on his seniority in that 
post counts from the dates he officiates and thercfore at the 
time of consideration of the question for promotion to the 
Dext higher post his seniority in the lower post is counted 
from the datc when he has officiated and not from the date 
when he is confirmed, Şuch being the position I am of the 
view that there is no justification for excluding the concerned 
workmen from being considered for promotion to the post of 
Clerk Gr. I by the D . P . C . in Arca No , V . In view of agrce 
ment between the management and the union sponsoring the 
cause of the concerned workman as per Ext, W - 3 the case 
of the concerned workman has to be considered for promo 
tion to Gr, I by the D . P . C . in Area No. V , in spite of the 
fact that he was transferred to Munidih Project in Decem 
her , 1976 . If after consideration the concerned workman is 
found fit for promotion to the next higher grade he should be 
promoted to that grade and given the pay of that grade. 
The action of the management therefore in transferring the 
concerned workman from Arca No . V prior to his being 
considered for promotion to Gr. I is not justified and go 
also the action of the management in not considering the 
case of concerned workman for promotion by tho D . P . C . 
The concerned workman is entitled to be considered for 
promotion to Go, I by the D . P . C . for Area No . V and is 
entitled to pay scale of a clerk in Gr. I in case he is found fit 


After the adoption of the policy for promotion & D . P . C . 
was formed by the management which considered several 
cases for promotion of clorks from one grade to other . It is 
admitted that the concerned workman was not considered by 
the D . P . C . as by the time of consideration the concerned 
workman had alrcady been transferred from Arca Office to 
Munidih Project - the transfer to Munidih Project being in 
December, 1976 . The case of the union is that there was no 
justification for the management not to consider the case of 
the concerned workman for promotion merely because 4 or 5 
months prior to the date of consideration of the question of 
promotion by the D . P . C . the concerned workman had been 
transferred to Muridih Project not at his own instance 
but by the management on administrative grounds. The further 
case of the union is that since th : concerned workman was 
entitled to be confirmed froni Alglist, 1973 he could not 
have come within the exception as provided in Para 1 of 
Ext, M - 1 and that the letter of confirmation confirming the 
concerned workman with effect from 6th December , 1974 ja 
a concoction to deprive the concerned workman of his right 
to be considered for promotion to the next higher grade. These 
contentions of the union are well founded , I have already 
stated that the union should have claimed confirmation for 
the workman with effect from 17th October, 1971 instend cf 
from August , 1973 . I have held above that the concerned 
workman is entitlcd for confirmation in the post of a clerk 
in Gr. II with effect from Augrist, 1973. It is not dj- puted 
that if the date of confirmation of the concerred workman is 
taken to be August, 1973 he would not come within the 
mischief of para 1 of Ext. M - 1 on the date of D .I . C . con : 
sidered cases of promotion . Reliance is placed on Ext. W - 3 by

7 . It will appear from the above that such workmen werd 
oven during the time of recommendation of the wago board 
were known as Goli Matti or Mud Pellet Mazdoors . Under 
wago board recommendation they were to be known as Clay 
Cartridges mazdoors in category I. This wage board recom 
mendation was accepted by the coal industry without any 
exception so that when the Government of India took over 
this colliery tho recommendation of the wage board had al 
ready been applied in all the collierica . So far as Ena col 
liery is concerned the written statement of the workmen has 
made specific plea that they have been serving in a gang in 
Ena colliery since January , 1971 . The evidence of WW - 1 is 
the same. The management has not denied it. MW - 1 has also 
not denied this. The management s contention is that instead 
of being workmen they were simply clay cartridge suppliers . 
It appears that the management of Ena colliery in January , 
1971 i.e , prior to the take over of the colliery by the Gov 
ernment of India had not put them as catcgory - I clay car 
tridge mazdoors which they should have done on implemen 
tation of the wage board recommendation . After the take 
over and nationalisation of Ena colliery on 1st May, 1972 
the erstwhile system continued . It is an admitted position 
that the Bharat Coking Coal Limited introduced uniform 
policy for all the mines and instructions were issued to pay 
category - I wagcs cven to the lowest type of workers . It ap 
poars that even this policy was not applied by the manage 
ment of Ena colliery in respect of these workers engaged 
in manufacture of clay cartridges. It is further an admitted 
position that such of the collieries where blaşting process was 
not introduced pick method was adopted , which means that 
the miners had themselves to cut the coal. Gradually between 
1972 and 1973 , all the collieries introduced the blasting pro 
cess and consequently , has to employ labourers for the pur 
pose of preparation and supply of clay cartridges , Shri Boso 
tells me that even after the introduction of blaşting system 
in such of the collieries where blasting system was newly 
introduced , tho workers engaged in preparation of clay car 
tridges were not designated as clay cartridgo mazdoors in 
category - I. The collierie , treated them as suppliers and pay 
ment used to be made to them at varying rates by such col 
Jieries . He further tells me that such workmen are now fac 
Ing unemployment because the collieries aro contemplating in 
troduction of machines for the purpose of manufacture of 
clay cartridges . This has created an abnormal situation so 
that a large number of disputes have been raised , and go 
far as this Court is concerned , we have three refer 
enccs before us which have been simultaneously heard , Shri 
Joshi tells me that several references on the same point are 
pending before other Tribunals algo , The position , therefore 
is that had the colliery management described them aq clay 
cartridge mazdoor, category - I at the time of nationalisation 
or even after the introduction of blasting system , as per re 
commendation of the wage board , no such dispute would 
have arisen . The question is whether there was any difficulty 
with tho collieries in designating them as category - I maz 
doors . It is here that we have to consider the evidence adduced 
on behalf of the parties .

we have to consider the evidence adduced on behalf of the 
parties

dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication on the following

SCHEDULE 
"Whether the demand of the workmen of Joenagore 

colliery of Messrs Bharat Coking Coal Limited 
Post office Khas Jeenagora , District Dhanbad tha 
the management should trcat Sarvashri Atul Mahato 
Pusti Mabalo , Panchu Bhuiya , Dukhni Mahatain 
Fulmani Mahatain , Lal Chandra , Rajnath Clay 
Cartridge Mazdoors as their workmen and that 
they should be paid category - I wage is justified : 
If so , to what relief are the said workmen cntitled 

and from what date ? " 
2 . The case of the workmen is that they were engaged by 
the management some time in the later part of 1975 for 
preparation of Clay cartridge at the colliery for using the 
same in the process of production of coal. Their further 
case is that they are entitled to be paid time rated wages 
in category I as described in the Coal wage board recommen 
dation which has been accepted by BCCL and implemented 
the same with improvement in subsequent wago agrcement 
But the management of Jeenagora colliery did not pay the 
concerned workmen category I rate of wages in the time 
scale inspite of repeated request by the workmen themselves 
as well as by their trade union . Consequently , the Union o 
the workmen represented their caso before the Agigtant 
Labour Commissioner ( C ) Dhanbad who took up the matter 
with the parties and held conciliation proceeding . Due to 
adament attitude of the management the conciliation ended 
in failure and ultimately this reference wag made by the 
Government of India , Ministry of Labour for adjudication to 
this Tribunal. Their further case is that the job performed 
by them at the colliery are for the benefit of the management 
and the service rendered for the benefit of the management 
and as such there is clear employer- employee relationship 
between them and the management. The prayer of the 
workmen is to regularise them in category - T from the date of 
their employment in the colliery in 1975. 


9 . Thus, having considered all aspects of the case, I hold 
that the demand of the workmen of North Tisra colliery of 
Messrs Bharat Coking Coal Limited , Post office Jeenagora , 
District Dhanbad that the management should treat Sarvashri 
Ram Swarup Paswan , Hira Yadav , Sughila Ravidasin , Rohans 
Rajwar, Bchari Bhuiya , Ganesh Bhuiya , Jogesh Bhuiya , 
Sadhu Bhuiya , Suraj Narain and Hari Narayan Singh , Clay 
Cartridge Mazdoors as their workmen and that they should be 
paid category -I wages is justified . Consequently , all the above 
workmen should be treated as the workmen of North Tisra 
colliery with effect from 1 - 5 - 1972 , and should be paid difference 
of category -I wages with effect from 1 - 5 - 1972 . They are also 
entitlcd to all the back wages and other emoluments with effect 
from 1 - 5 - 1972 . 
This is my award . 

J. P . SINGH, Presiding Officer.

2 . In pursuance of this reference a notice was issuod to 
the parties while the workman put in his appearance through 
his union , the Management did not put in any appearanco 
inspite of service . Io view thereof ex - parte proceedings were 
ordered against the Management and ex- parte evidenco of 
the workman was recorded . The ex -parto evidence of the 
workman consists of his statement as W . W . 1 apart from 
documents Ex, W / 1 and Ex, W / 2 . Ex , W / 1 is the letter 
of retrenchment and Ex , W / 2 is the senjority list. The state 
ment of the workman reads as under : 


I am working as Jeep Driver since 21 - 1 - 1974 with the 

Iespondent No. 2 . The respondent No. 2 vido 
letter Ex. W / l retrenchment my services . Though 
the two junior persons were continued in service . 
There were five workmen working as Driver as per 
annexurc Ex, W / 2 . I was made illegally junior by 
retrenching the workman at serial No . 5 shown 
in Ex. W / 2 and the workman at sl. No . 4 in Ex, 
W / 2 has been transferred to the cadre of X . E . N . 
III though there were no specific orders of the 
Competent Authority for transferring the post from 
one cadre 10 another. The reasons mentioned in 
the termination notice as reduction in cadro is alon 
incorrect as there are no orders of reduction in 
cadre of the post of Driver from the Competent 
Authority . I have not been paid the retrenchment 
compensation 60 far. The respondents has not 
followed the principle of last come first go . 


3 . The contention of the workman is that one Shri Abdul 
Gaffur 6 / 0 Shri Alimchand who was junior to him was 
retained in service while the workman was retrenched . From 
the perusal of seniority list I do find that Shri Abdul Gaffur 
was junior as he was recruited on 22 - 5 -78 as against Shri 
Om Parkash , the concerned workman who was recruited on 
21- 1- 74 , as a parusal of Ex. W / 2 would show . In view of 
these facts it follows that the management has not complied 
with the principle of last come first go while retrenching the 
workman Om Parkash and consequently I hold that jo view 
of the evidence produced by the workman it is established 
that the order of retrenchment of workman , Shri Om Par 
kash , Jeep Driver with effect fron 10th November , 1979 
is not justified and the workman is deemed to be continuing 
in service on the same terms and conditions. Accordingly 
it is awarded that the action of Fexcutive Engineer (Survey 
and Construction )- II , Western Railway , Kota , in torminating 
the services of Shri Om Parkash , Jeep Driver, with effect

7. On the basis of the aforesaid findings the concerned 
workman was dismissed from service with the Bank with 
effect from 1st July , 1976 by the appropriate authority by a 
notice dated 19th July , 1976 . Thereafter he proferred an 
appeal before the Appellato Authority appointed by the Bank . 
The said appcal was duly heard . In the concluding portion 
of his findings the Appellate Officer stated as follows : " Taking 
into consideration all factors , I and that the employee, by 
his conduct has made himself unaccoptable to the bank s 
service discipline, in the public interost. However, considering 
the nature of the offence committed, though it is gross is 
conduct and serious from tho administrative point of vlow , 
I still hold the vicw that he can be given an opportunity to 
dock service elsewhere and promoto his future livelihood. In 
this context, I feel inclined to reduce the punishment to ono 
of discharge without notice, in terms of paragraph 521( 10 ) (c ) 
of the Sastry Award as retained in the Degal Tribunal, instead 
of dismissal as proposed by the Disciplinary Authority. " Tho 
punishment was therefore reduced to ono of a discharge 
without notico in terms of paragraph 521( 10 )(c ) of the Sastry 
Award . Accordingly , by in order dated 26th November, 1976 
the concerned workman was discharged from tho bank s 
service . 


8 . After the said order was intimated to the concerned 
workman he was paid his statutory dues and he accepted the 
game. 


9 . It is pleaded in paragraph 15 of tho bank s written state 
ment that the bank wag entitled to dismiss the concerned 
workman under the provisions of the Sastry Award and the 
departmental proceedings were conducted strictly in accordance 
with the provisions of the said award . 


10 . By its pleadings the bank took a preliminary objoction , 
namely , that the dispute has neither been sponsored nor has 
it been espoused by a substantial number of workmen and 
the referenco is therefore Invalid and incompetent and tho 
Tribunal has no jurisdiction to entertain the same. 


11 . Having regard to the provisions of Section 2A of tho 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 the objection does not appear 
to be valid . It is true that the order of reference does not 
refer to Soction 2A of the Industrial Dispute Act. It merely 
says that the dispute has been referred to the Tribunal under 
clause ( d ) of Sub - section ( 1 ) of Section 10 of tho Industrial 
Disputes Act , 1947 . Section 2A does not confer any power to 
refer an industrial dispute to a tribunal for adjudication , 
The section merely converts a disputo which is not an indus 
trial dispute into an industrial dispute under certain circum 
stances . The omission of any mention of Section 2A in the 
order of referenco , in my opinion , does not vitiate a reforence 
if Section 2A is applicable in the facts and circumstances , 
as it is the present case . 


ORDBR 

Dated, 27th December, 1979 
Government of India , Ministry of Labour , by thcir Order 
No , L - 12012 / 140 / 77- D . ILA dated 21st / 24th October, 1978 
referred the following dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal ; 
" Whether the action of the management of the State 

Bank of India , Calcutta Local Head Office , Jeevan 
Deep Building, 1 , Middleton Row , Calcutta - 700016 
in discharging Shri Ram Laik Singh , Messenger from 
service with effect from 19 - 7 - 76 is justified ? If 

not, to what relief is the workman entitled ? " 
2 . The concerned workman Sri Ram Laik Singh , a Messen 
ger in the employment of the Bank at its Netaji Subhas Road 
Branch was permitted , among other members of the sub 
Ordinate staff on duty to live temporarily in the Bank s "Garad" 
and was not provided with residential accommodation . Tho 
case as pleadod by the management is that it was decided by 
tho management to shift those persons who were putting up 
at the " Garad" at the time of renovation of the bank premises, 
from the Bank s Garad to the second floor in Block D . 
Thereupon the bank duly notified to the workmen concerned 
for making thoir respective arrangements for the said shift 
by an office order dated June 26 , 1972, Ext. M - 4 . Pursuant 
to the aforesaid order the members of the subordinate staff 
except the concernod workman and three others shifted by 
removing their belongings to block D . The concerned work 
man chose to remain in the Garad in disobedience to the 
said order and in spite of repeated requests made by his 
superiors in that behalf. 

3 . Thereafter on or about March 2 , 1974 when the work 
for shifting of the Security dopartment under the said arrange 
ments were started under the supervision of the Bank officials 
to the place where the said Garad was previously situated , 
tho concerned workman unluwfully obstructed the work of 
the said bank . While the then Manager intervened and reques 
ted him to shift, be was humiliated with abusive language by 
the said workman who oven threatened the coolies who had 
teen hired for the purpose of the said work with dire conse 
quences . As a result, work had to be suspended for the 
time being 

4 . On or about 14th March , 1974 the concerned workman 
was requested in writing to remove his belongings to the said 
block D . In disobedience to the said order he resisted the 
bank s shifting programme and unlawfully removed certain 
furniture of the bank and placed his own cots in the Garad. 

5 . In the premises aforesaid , for this act of gross mis 
conduct he was placed under suspension on or about March 
19 , 1974 and was duly served with a charge sheet dated 28th 
August, 1974 . 

6 . Thereupon an enquiry Officer was duly appointed and 
he conducted an enquiry . He found the said workman guilty 
of the charges. The enquiry proceedings and the Renort and 
Findings of the Enquiry Officer have been annexed to the 
written statement filed on behalf of the management before 
this Tribunal and marked with the letter A .

Sir , 


" Now , the exercise of disciplinary powers, in the field 

disciplinary action , is not confined merely to the 
passing by the appointing authority of an ultimato 
order imposing disciplinary punishment against tho 
omployec . It extends oven to the very initiation of 
disciplinary action against a civil servant or emp 
toyoo by framing charges against him and holding , 
or directing the holding of an enquiry into these 
charges. The framing of charges , tho holding of an 
enqutry into them , the suspansion of the civil ser 
vant during the enquiry , tho notice to show cause , 
Aro all steps in the exercise of the disciplinary power . 
These stops must be taken by the disciplinary autho 
rity and not by a delegate of that authority .

the case of Indian Explosives Ltd , v Presiding Oncer , Labour 
Court, Patnu , 1975 Lab . I. C . 641 topt whero a domcetic 
enquiry was held against the workman for arraulting another 
workman and the material Allegations were sufficiently dis 
closed in the chargesheet and the report lodged to the Police 
was read over to him and exhibited in the case and he examin 
ed a large number of witnesses in his defence without any 
grievance that copy of complaint received by the emplover 
was not innlied to him . Ibi non - production of that copy did 
not prejurlice the workman os vitiate the enguiry for 
violation of the principles of natural justice . In the present 
case the material allegations Vero sufficiently disclosed in 


Learned counsel also relied on a docision of the Assam 
High Court in Manihar Singh v Superintendent of Police, 
United Khasi and Jaintia Huis, 1969 II LLJ , P . 493, whero 
it was held that in the absence of a statutory provision 
permitting expressly or impliedly delegation of disciplinary 
Powers , an authority other than the disciplinary authority 
has clearly no power to framo on its own initiative , charges 
against a civil servant and hold enquiry into them . It is 
to bo noted that these judgmente relate to disciplinary action 
taken against civil servants under Article 311 of the 
Constitution of India and Government Servants Conduct 
Rules . The Sastry Award is very largely a selt contained Code 
and whether it is obligatory under the Sastry award for the 
officers competent to take disciplinary action to issue charge 
shects as well , has to be answered on the basis of Award 
itself. 


24 . Clause 520 of the Sastry award stateg : " Under the 
subject of disciplinary action we deal with dismissal, guspen 
sion , warning or censure , fine, the making of adverse remarks 
and the stoppago of an increment." In this clause discipli 
nary proceedings including iggue of chargesheets are not stated 
to be a part of disciplinary action , Clause 521 states that 
a person against whom disciplinary action is proposed or 
likely to be taken , should , in the first instance, be informed 
of the particulars of the charge against him ; he should have 
a proper opportunity to give his explanation as to such 
particulars. In other words, a chargesheet precedes discipli 
nary action and is therefore no part of disciplinary action . 
The position is made even clearer in sub - clause ( a ) of clause 
( 10 ) of paragraph 521. There it is provided that an employce 
against whom disciplinary action is proposed or likely to be 
taken shall be given a chargesucet. In the contemplation of 


(917 II - - 


3 (ii)] 


Fita T 69 : A14 14 , 1981/ F187 23 , 1902 


905 


this provision a chargesheot is separate from disciplinary 
Action . Having regard to the clear languago and the spirit 
of the relevant provision of paragraphs 520 and 521 of the 
Sastry award I am of opinion that issue of a chargcsheet is a 
part of disciplinary proccodings and not of disciplinary action 
in the context of the Sastry award . It is therefore not 
necessary that tho officer empowered to tako disciplinary action 
should alone issue a chargesheet. In thut view of the matter, 
I hold that the chargesheet which was issucd by the 
Branch Manager was validly issued .

The Bank in its rejoinder to the workman s statement of 
claim içiterated the averments made in their written state 
meat and denied the several allcgations made by the workman .

statement filed on behalf of the Bank . 


The issues that arise for consideration in this case on the 
above pleadings are : 
1 . Whether the domestic enquiry js vitiated on account 

of the bias of the Enquiry Officer or on account of 

the wrong procedure followed by him ? 
2 . Whether the findings of the Enquiry Officer are per 

verse and not based on proper evidence ? 
3 . Whether the punishment of dismissal is dispropor 

tionate to the charges proved against him ? 
4 . Whether the workman is entitled to reinstatement with 

full back wages ? 
5. To what relief ? 


Issue 1 : 


Shri Gadkare for the workman docs not say that the enz 
quiry is vitiated on account of any violation of the princi 
ples of natural justice . It is also not his case that the En 
quiry Officer is any way personally biased against the 
workman . According to him , the following instance prove 
the Enquiry Officer s bias against the workman . While the 
Enquiry Officer pcrmitted the management to elicit certain 
further information from their witness Mr. Heredia by way 
of re - examination he did not allow the workman to CTOS 
cxamine the witness on those points . This is said to have 
caused grave prejudice to the workman . I do not agree . The 
workman who conducted his defence in person did not appear 
to have sought permission to cross examine the witness on 
the points elicited in the re - examination . The facts spoken to 
in re - examination are that on 1 - 12 - 75 the workman camo 
drunk to the office at 4 .05 P . M , after having left for lunch 
at 1 . 30 PM , True this fact was not spoken to by the witness 
in his chief examination . Thc charge itcm 20 does not also 
allege that the workman hud returned from lunch drink . It 
merely refers to his having left for lunch at 1. 30 P . M . and 
returned late at 4 .05 P . M . The Enquiry Officer in his report 
at page 7 doce not appear to have taken this information 
sought in tho re - cxamination in considering the case against 
the workman . Therefore it cannot be said that any prejudice 
has been caused to the wrokmas on this account. This in 
formation clicited in the re - exumination shall stand excluded . 
In the re - examination of the same witness Mr. Heredia ho 
stated that the workman did not complete the work on cer 
tain dates mentioned therein . This piece of evidence also was 
not taken into account by the Enquiry Ollicer in considering 
the guilt of the workman. No prejudice therefore can be said 
to have been caused to the workman on this score . This 
portion of the evidence may also be excluded . During tho 
cross examination of Mr, Harçdia lhe workman asked him 
to produce his personali diary wherein he had noted tho 
several dates with time on which the workman attended the 
office late . The diary was not made available to the workman 
though at the close of the enquiry proceedings it appears to 
have been filed before the Enquiry Officer. How the workman 
has been prejudiced on this account is not made clear. The 
late comings of the workman are noted not only in the diary 
but also in the mustor roll which was placed on the record . 
The suggestion made to Mr. Heredia is that in his personal 
diary he was noting the time of arrival of the workman and 
Mr. Alfanso only and not that of tho other employees. 
Mr, Heredia justified this action by saying that he an 
prchcaded that the timings noted in the muster against the 
game to individual might be tempered with , In the light of 
this evidence it can not be said that any prejudice is caused 
to the workman by the personal diary of Mr. Heredia not 
being made available to the workman and its being placed 
hefore the Enquiry Officer after the close of the evidence. 
Again during the cross cxamination of Mr. Heredia , the 
workman asked him whether his signature appoared in the 
day book , on certain dates, with a view to show that it was 
the Manuger that chrcked the dny book on the dates his 
signature appeared thereon and not the workman , The 
Manager s ytalement is that after checking the day book with 
reference to the general ledger he would sign the day book 


and initial the ledger and that the pregerxe of his signaturo 
in tho day book on any date did not show that be caecvd 
it. I do not see how in the jight of the Manager s evidence 
the question disallowed would have helped the workman in 
establishing his case that it was the Manager that gcnerally 
checked the day book . 

len the workman asked the Manager Mr. Heredia 
whether he did not over look his claims to be entrusted with 
the functions of a Special Assistant while he (Heredia ) was 
working as an Accountant and the workman as # Cleib in 
Margao branch . The Enquiry Officer held that this question 
was not relevant to the issues involved in the enquiry . 
Mr, Gadkare for the workman submitted that by disallowing 
this question the Enquiry Officer deprived the workman of 
A valuable opportunity of proving the prejudice the Manager 
had against him . I do not think that the workman was in any 
way adversely affected by this question being disallowed . 

Lastly , during the cross -examination of Narveknr the 
Peon the workmin asked him whotlier the route to the post 
office from the Bank via the Sangucm Bar was the shortest. 
This quesion was disallowed us such was not big statement 
in the chief examination. To appreciato this point il may 
be noticed that on 28 - 11- 75 the workman is alleged to have 
been entrusled with the duty of balancing the No . 2 S . B . 
ledger . Instead of doing this work be is said to have left the 
bank at 1.30 P . M . for lunch and never returned . The Manager 
who had to attend a conference at Panjim or Poona could 
not loave the station because of the workman s failure to 
completo the work entrusted to him . The Manager asked the 
pcon the same day at about 5 .00 P . M . to send a telegram to 
the concernect authorities expressing his inability to attend the 
conference . As the peon was passing by the side of the 
Sanguem Bar Alfanso another Clerk of this Bank look the 
pcon inside the bar where according to the peon ht found 
the workman . It is the further case of the management that 
the workman snatched the filled in telegraph form from the 
peon and tore it to pieces . In order to improbeblise this ver 
sion the peon was sought to be asked in his cross -examina 
tion whether it was necessary to pass by the side of the San 
gliem Bar lo go to tho post office by tho shortest route . This 
line of cross examination was disallowed because the odon 
never stated in his chief that he took the nearest route to go 
to the post office. May be the question could bave been allowed 
in the cross examination but 11o prejudice can bo said to have 
been caused to the workman by same being disallowed. The 
next infirmity in the enquiry proceedinge pointed out is tho 
management examined 4 witnesses by namcs M . Pereira , 
Salelkar, S . P . Narvelkur und Dr. Nadkarni to show that on 
ono day the workman was found drunk in the company of 
Alfanso and that they helped both the drunks to reach home. 
This evidence was adduced to show tbat by his conduct tho 
workman was bringing down the reputation of the Bank . 
Il is argued that this conduct of his evon jf true , was beyond 
the scope of the enquiry as the same is not the subject matter 
of any of the charges . There is some substance in this com 
plaint and the Enquiry Officer at page 5 of his Andings of 
the personal hearing granted to him after the second show 
Cause notice observes that the evidence of the aforesaid wit 
nesses was connected with the allegations made in the 
charge sheet. He added that the workman was found guilty 
" Indercndent of this evidence" . The cvidence of these four 
witnesses in my view should be excluded in considering the 
guilt of the workman . 

For the aforesaid reasons issue 1 held against the workmou .

Point 1 found accordingly. 
Issue 2 : 


The workmun herein joined the service of the Bank in 
1966 as 2 Clerk . From 1969 to 1978 he worked in the 
Goa Region which is one of the 15 regions into which the 
area of operations of the Bank is divided . According to 
seniority Clerks are posted as Special Assistants which 
Currics a special functional allowance . The appointment to 
the post of Special Assistant is governed by the terms of the 
Agreement datcd 24 - 12 - 1971 (Ex . W - 1 ). A clerk who has 
completed six years of service as a clerk in the Bank is 
cligible to be entrusted with the duties of Special Assistant. 
Seniority list is to be prepared on the basis of length of 
service in the Bank half- ycarly , once on 30th September and 
again on 31st March euch year. The list prepared on 31st 
March will be operative from 1st April to 30th September 
and the list prepared on 30th September will be operative 
from 1st October to 31st March . In considering the senjority 
of the clerks weightage is to be given to the educational 
qualifications like Graduation /National Diploma in Com 
merce /CAIIB /CAIIB Part 1 / CAIB Part II. If a clerk 
refuses the post of the Special Assistant for whatever reason 
hc will be given the first available chance after completion 
of one year from the date of refusal of the chance . if he 
refuses the offer for a second time he will be given a third 
and final chance after completion of one year from the 
second refusal. If the Clerk reflises the third and final 
chancc also he shall be placed at the bottom of the list of 
that region . If a vacancy for Special Assistant arose in a 
region and if there is no eligible candidate in that region , 
the management may javite applications from eligible can 
didates from other regions and posting will be made from 
among such volunteers . As per the seniority list effective 
from 1- 10 - 1973 the workman was offered on 21 - 1 - 1974 the 
post of Special Assistant that fell vacant at Pale Branch . In 
the course of his evidence WW - 1 stated that he had to declino 
that offer as Pale town had no telephone facility . Ho further 
stated that by 21- 1 - 1975 he was the General Secretary , 
Goa Bank Employees Association which was an organisa 
tion of all the Bank employees and a member of Central 
Committee of All India Bank Employees Association which 
was the then sole bargaining agent of the Bank Employees 
and Vice -President Syndicate Bank Employees Association 
which was the recognised union of the Syndicate Bank . Ho 
felt that going to Pale would seriously bamper his trade 
union activity . There is no dispute about the workman being 
offered the vacancy at Palo . On 5 - 3 - 1975 a post of Special 
Assistant fell vacant at Merces which is only three miles 
away from Panaji . By 5 - 3 - 1975 the workman says he was 
the senior moat clerk in the seniority list that was then current 
je, the list of 30 - 9 - 1974 , a copy of which is filed as Ex. M - 2 
(page No. 10 ) which shows that the workman was placed at 
S . No. 3 in the seniority ligt. Mrs . Chhaya V . Kapado aod 
Messrs Anit S , Gadgkar were the two persone senior to him . 
The rogt at Merces was filled up on 7 - 4 - 1975 in accordance 
with the fresh senjority list prepared on 31- 3 - 1975 which is 
Ex . M - 7 . By 1 - 4 - 1975 Mrs . Kapade continued to remain 
first in the list. The second in the list was Miss Shanta G . 
Deshpande, who is a B . Com .. CAIB . The third was 
MTA . Anit S . Gadgkar and the fourth was the workman 
himself . During the course of his evidence the workman 
os WW - 1 atated that the lists Ex M - 2 and M - 1 were corroct. 
As per the list Ex. M - 2 he says he woud be the 
senior most eligible candidate for entrustment of Special 
Assistant dutics by 5 - 3 - 75, the date on which the 
vacancy at Merces arose. According to him by 5 -3 - 1975 
Mrs. Kanade was posted AS Special Assistant at 
Rihander Branch and the second senior -most Mrs. Gadgkar 
had alrendy given the letter dated 6 - 11 - 1973 (Px, W - 5 ) to 
the Chairman and Managing Director , Syndicate Bank . 
Manipur expressing her unwillingnege to serve as Srecjal 
Assistant at any place other than Vasco - da -Gama in view of 
her family difficulties . He was thus the next seniormoat to 
he offered this post at Merces as per the seniority list dated 
30 - 9 - 74 . He also contends that in filling up a post of 
Special Assistant the Bank should be guided by the geniority 
list that is current by the date the vacancy actually arose and 
not the scnlority list current on the date the vacancy is sought 
1177 GI/ 80 - 8 . 


to be filled up . In this case it is not disputed that the vacancy 
was filled up in April, 1975 following the senjority list pre 
pared on 30 - 3 - 1975, Thc Bank disputes tho claim of the 
workman on two grounds viz. that aven if the seniority list 
current on 5 - 3 - 1973 was followed the workman was not the 
senior-most. Mrs. Kapade and Gadgkar will be above him . 
The Bank also says that it is the seniority list that is effective 
on the date the vacancy is sought to be filled up that should 
govern and not the list current on the date the vacancy arose . 
He also says that in view of the inordinate delay in raising 
the dispute relating to the filling up of the Merces Branch 
Vacancy the workman cannot be permitted to agitato the 
same. 

The Agreement Ex . M - 1 is silent on the question as to 
which seniority list should be followed in filling up the 
vacancy of a Special Assistant, whether the seniority list 
current on the date the post foll vacant or tho seniority list 
current on the date on which it is proposed to be filled up . 
In the absence of any guidelines being found in Ex. W - 1 in 
tbj, regard the Bank bonafide felt that it was the list in 
force on the date the vacancy is sought to be filled up that 
should govern and not the earlier list. The view taken by the 
Bank cannot be questioned as being unreasonable or unjust. 
The workman s allegation that the Bank doliberately did not 
All up the vacancy on the date the vacancy arose with a view 
to victimise him does not seem to be justified . There is no 
evidence whatsoever that from the date the agreement Ex 
W - 1 came into force i.e . 24 - 12 - 1971 the Bank hag deviated 
from the stand they took in the case of filling up the vacancy 
that arose OD 5 - 3 - 1975 . In the absence of a specific direction 
in the agreement Ex. W - 1 the management is justified in 
exercising its discretion in following the list that was in force 
at the timc of filling up the post. The argument that the 
list that was in force on the date the vacancy aroge should 
be applicable may also be plausible . But when the Bank hag 
consistently taken the other view which is also equally plat 
sible. I see no point in substituting my judgement for theirs 
in the absence of any malafide being established . The Bank 
contends that even if the seniority list Ex. M - 1 dated 
30 - 9 - 74 is to govern the workman will not be the senior-most 
on 5 -3 - 1975. Mrs . Kapado and Mrs . Gadgkar are senior to 
him , The workman s cage is that Mrs . Kapade has already 
been posted as Special Assistant at Ribadder Branch . The 
learned Advocale for the management contends that till 
19-8 - 1976 Mrs. Kapade continued to be a clerk . They rely 
upon a copy of the order of even date posting Mrs . Kapado 
as Special Assistant at Ribander branch . So the claim of the 
workman that he was the senior -most as per the list Ex. M - 1 
dated 30 - 9 - 1974 cannot be correct . Then there is Mrs . Gadgkar 
above him in the list. The workman as W - 1 did not disputo 
the correctness of the list Ex, M - 1 showing Mrs. Kadgkar as 
senior to him . On 20 - 1- 1981 he filed a Memo. requesting 
the Court to call upon the management to produce 5 documents 
mentioned therein to say in the seniority list Ex . M - 1 Mrs . 
Gadckar was wrongly shown as senfor to him . He also says 
that he did not know about this flaw in the seniority list 
Ex. M - 1 till after the date of his deposition . The workman 
did not protest from 1975 till 1978 against the management s 
filling up the most at Merces by following the seniority list 
of 31- 3 - 1975 , The workman having acquiesced in the manage 
ment s action from 1 -4 - 1975 he shall not ble permitted 
to reopen that question in 1978 . If his contention is to bo 
unheld it will have the effect of unsettling the appointments 
made from the date Ex , WI agreciment became effective , 
Ti is then argued that tho fact that the management waited 
till 7 -4 - 1975 to fill in the vacancy at Morces the malafideg 
of the management should be inferred . The management s 
counsel Shri Pai gubmits that the Management cannot he 
expected to fill up this post within a dav or two after tho 
Vacancy aroso . Tt takes some time for the management to 
move in the normal courec . Further even if the mannement 
wanted to fill up that most without delav they should first 
offer the post to Mrs. Kapade , the senior-most candidata 
und waited for her letter of anntoval. Even the workman took 
more than one month to rejcct the offer of Special Assis 
tant s post at Pale . 

For the aforesaid reasons I hold that the action of the 
Bank in filling tip the vacancy of Snecial Assistant at Merceg 
as per the seniority list dated 31 - 3 - 1975 is not malande. 

The learned counsel for the workman then contended that 
the seniority list dated 30 - 9 - 1974 is wrong 90 far as it 
shows Mrs . Gadekar as senjor to him . To establish this fact 
hr filed a netition on 21- 1 - 1981 praying the Court to direct 
the management to file certain documents . The workman 
submitted that he had come to know of the defectivo 
nature of that senlority list only in November , 1980 and so
